Multiple Choice Examination 

Following each statement are five possible answers. Only ONE is correct. Circle that ONE in proper place. 

1. A search warrant may be served: (1) At any time; (2) By any person; (3) By the officer to whom it is directed; (4) Within 30 days of the time it is issued; (5) By the complaining witness.- 1 2 3 4 5. 1 

2. Corpus delicti means: (1) Proving the defendant guilty; (2) Producing the victim's body at a murder trial; (3) Proving that an act was committed unlawfully by a criminal agency; (4) The preparation of testimony for the inquest; (5) The motive involved in any crime.-1 2 3 4 5. 

3. Bench warrants are commonly issued when: (1) A witness in a civil action has demanded his fees; (2) When any person has disobeyed any lawful order of any court; (3) A person has resisted arrest or insulted an officer; (4) A prisoner refuses to put up bond after bail is granted him; (5) An officer testifies in behalf of the defendant in a criminal trial.-1 2 3 4 5. 

4. The term "probation" means: (1) Giving time off for good behavior; (2) Releasing a prisoner from the state penitentiary after serving the minimum time imposed in his sentence; (3) Allowing the defendant his freedom before trial without putting up bail; (4) The release of a person convicted of crime before any part of his sentence has been served; (5) Investigation by the court of the defendant's background and personal habits.-1 2 3 4 5. 

5. If the mayor of a city refuses to perform any legal duty the correct procedure to force him to perform this duty is: (1) Institute proceedings for his recall; (2) Present a petition to the city council; (3) Have him impeached; (4) Sue out a writ of mandamus; (5) Take it up with the women's clubs.-1 2 3 4 5. 

6. Penology is the science of study of: (1) Criminal prevention; (2) 

Prisons; (3) Rehabilitation and punishment of criminals; (4) 

Criminal psychology; (5) Code penalties.-1 2 3 4 5. 

7. Released O.R., good time, repeaters, three time losers, are terms that refer to: (1) Civil litigants; (2) Writs and processes; (3) Criminals; (4) Legal documents; (5) Pawnbrokers requirements.- 1 2 3 4 5.. 

8. The criminal subpoena is: (1) The process of requiring a witness to appear in court; (2) A warrant of arrest; (3) Notification that action has been brought against the defendant; (4) A paper permitting the officer to search the premises; (5) The commitment to the county jail.-1 2 3 4 5.

9. Under the general term "theft" is the old term "emblezzlement," which means: (1) Selling worthless stock; (2) Obtaining money by threat of future violence; (3) Cashing fictitious checks; (4)

Fraudulent appropriation of property intrusted to a person; (5) 

Picking pockets.-1 2 3 4 5. 

10. If a malicious defamation of a person living or dead is printed in a newspaper, it is a crime, and this crime is known as: (1) Common barratry; (2) Criminal slander; (3) Extortion; (4) Subornation of perjury; (5) Criminal libel.-1 2 3 4 5. 

11. X, a fire-bug, maliciously and intentionally sets fire to and destroys a barn. X had no intention of harming any person but unbeknownst to X, T, a tramp, is sleeping inside the barn and is killed in the fire. This would be: (1) Involuntary manslaugter; (2) Accidental homicide; (3) Second degree murder; (4) 

Mayhem; (5) First degree murder.-1 2 3 4 5. 

12. Killing a person by the use of poison is: (1) Voluntary manuslaughter; (2) First degree murder; (3) Excusable homicide; (4) 

Second degree murder; (5) Negligent homicide.-1 2 3 4 5. 

13. According to the laws, one of the following marriages would be declared incestuous and void; a marriage between; (1) Son-in-law and mother-in-law; (2) Ancestors and descendants; (3) stepdaughter and step-father; (4) Divorced parents; (5) Adopted children and foster parents.-1 2 3 4 5. 14. 

On January 15, 1939, A shot her husband, B, in the abdomen. 

On January 26, 1940, B died, his death evidently being hurried by the wound received. A is guilty of: (1) First degree murder; (2) Second degree murder; (3) Assault with a deadly weapon; (4) Voluntary manslaughter; (5) Res ipsa loquitor.-1 2 3 4 5. 

15. O, an officer, in order to get evidence on J, pretends to assist J in the burglary of R's house. This activity on O's part is known as: (1) Inveiglement; (2) Entrapment; (3) Concealment; (4) Enticement; (5) Seduction.-1 2 3 4 5. 

16. A says to B, "See that man? He is a millionaire. Give me $500 and I'll make you wealthy." B gives A $500. A's statements are false and A intended to defraud B. (1) We haven't any crime here; (2) False pretenses; (3) Larceny by trick; (4) Embezzlement; (5) Bribery.-1 2 3 4 5. 

17. X pretends to Y that he has perfected a machine that will counterfeit money and induces Y to get a thousand dollars so that he may use it to 'print' some money in the machine. Y gives the $1000 to X for that purpose and X decamps with Y's money. This constitutes: (1) Larceny; (2) Embezzlement; (3) False pretenses; (4) False cheats; (5) Champerty.-1 2 3 4 5. 

18. X, driving an auto recklessly, crashed into Y's automobile. X is

badly hurt, and demands that Y take him to a hospital. Y drives away leaving X at the scene of colision. Y was slightly hurt. (1) This leaves Y in a bad way; (2) X has no claim on Y, as it was A's fault; (3) X alone has a duty to perform towards Y; (4) 

Y can have X arrested for reckless driving. Y cannot be held; (5) X is guilty of negligent homicide.-1 2 3 4 5. 

19. "The unlawful violation of the personal liberty of another" is called: (1) Kidnapping; (2) Abduction; (3) Rout; (4) False imprisonment; (5) Procuring.-1 2 3 4 5. 

20. "Any use of force or violence, disturbing the public peace, or any threat to use such violence, if accompanied by immediate power of execution by two or more persons working together, and without authority of law," is: (1) A rout; (2) A riot; (3) An unlawful assembly; (4) A lynching; (5) An extradition.-1 2 3 4 5. 

21. A woman came to the police station and voluntarily confessed to the murder of a baby born to her several years before. This woman could: (1) Be convicted on her voluntary confession; (2)

Not be convicted unless the body of the baby was found; (3) Be convicted even though shown to be insane; (4) Not be convicted until the evidence was secured to prove that a murder, such as she described was in fact committed; (5) Subjected to sobriety test.-1 2 3 4 5. 

22. A and B at different times entered the barnyard of C intending to steal. A took a calf worth approximately ten dollars and B took four hens worth about the same amount: (1) A and B are both guilty of grand theft; (2) A only is guilty of grand theft; (3) B only is guilty of grand theft; (4) A is guilty only of petty theft; (5) Neither is guilty of a crime.-1 2 3 4 5. 

23. "Every person who is a drug addict" on taking the drug under the direction of a licensed physician, is guilty of: (1) Vagrancy; 

(2) Violating the Harrison Narcotic Act; (3) Violating the Pure 

Food and Drug Act; (4) Possession; (5) Criminal Syndicalism.- 1 2 3 4 5. 

24. A girl of 15 consented to leave her home with a woman acquaintance to enter a house of prostitution. The parents of the girl objected, but, by trickery, the woman managed to get the girl away from her home. The woman should be prosecuted for: (1) 

Seduction; (2) Abduction; (3) Kidnapping; (4) Adultery; (5)

Champerty.-1 2 3 4 5. 

25. When a person receives or buys property from a minor under 13, not at a fixed place of business carried on by such minor or his employer, said property is: (1) The Property of the buyer; (2) Disposed of legally; (3) Presumed to be stolen; (4) Liable for garnishment; (5) Subject to quo warranto.-1 2 3 4 5.

26. "A person who is allowed to go outside the buildings and inclosures of a state prison, who is still under legal custody and control and subject to being returned at any time," is said to be: (1) Released; (2) On parole; (3) Pardoned; (4) On probation; (5) Condemned.-1 2 3 4 5. 

27. X gave his 14-year-old son, S, an old auto. S found he could not make the auto run so wrecked it and sold the Junk to T, a local junk dealer. (1) T has committed a crime in buying the junk from S; (2) T was licensed junk dealer so could buy junk wherever it was available; (3) As X gave the auto to S, both X and T are guilty of misdemeanor; (4) The facts as stated would not constitute a crime; (5) is presumed guilty of receiving stolen goods. -1 2 3 4 5. 

28. A gave X a necessarily fatal dose of poison. B found X languishing from the poison and cut off X's head. (1) A may be convicted of attempted murder; (2) Neither A nor B can be convicted; (3) 

Both A and B are criminally responsible for X's death; (4) As 

X will die anyway, B cannot be held; (5) B should be declared insane.-1 2 3 4 5. 

29. W, a healthy pregnant woman, asks D to perform an abortion. 

D, intending to cause an abortion, administers a drug which causes the child to be born prematurely. The child dies due to premature birth. D is arrested. (1) D may be only prosecuted for an attempted abortion; (2) This is second degree murder; (3) This is first degree murder; (4) This is manslaughter; (5) No crime has been committed.-1 2 3 4 5. 

30. In a crowded darkened theatre X cried "fire" causing a panic in which Y is trampled to death. (1) Just a foolish prank for which

X cannot be held criminally responsible; (2) X is guilty of first degree murder; (3) X is guilty of second degree murder; (4) X is guilty of manslaughter; (5) Other facts and circumstances and 

X's intent will have to be inquired into before it can be established whether or not X is criminally liable.-1 2 3 4 5. 

31. A, an expert swimmer, a stranger to B, sits on a wharf and does nothing while he watches B drown. (1) Manslaughter; (2) Second degree murder; (3) Mayhem; (4) First degree murder; (5)

Nothing criminal.-1 2 3 4 5. 

32. In order to elicit sympathy and thus to collect alms by begging, B solicits X to cut off B's hand. X does so. B dies from loss of blood. (1) As B consented, X is not liable; (2) This is involuntary monslaughter; (3) This is murder; (4) This is excusable homicide; (5) This is justifiable homicide.-1 2 3 4 5. 

33. A finds B with his wife and kills B. (1) A may set up the unwritten law as a defense; (2) The unwritten law cannot be in-

voked as a defense in such a case; (3) The unwritten law gives a man the right to avenge a wrong; (4) There is no such meaning as the right to avenge a wrong applied to the phrase, "the unwritten law;" (5) Common law criminal prosecution would apply here.-1 2 3 4 5. 

34. The statute of limitations for a felony other than murder, embezzlement of public money, or for the falsification of public records is: (1) Six months; (2) One year; (3) 2 years; (4) 3 years; (5) 5 years.-1 2 3 4 5. 

35. A, a married man, knowing his marriage to still be legal, and undissolved, decides to marry another woman. B, a friend of A's, having full knowledge of the existence of A's legal marriage status, helps A to secure the marriage license and in other ways assists him in the planning of the second marriage. The second marriage is actually performed with B acting as best man. B may be: (1) Held as accessory before the fact; (2) Convicted of bigamy; (3) Excused because he thought it was a joke; (4) Charged with fraud and deceit; (5) Subject to a civil suit for damages arising from malicious mischief.-1 2 3 4 5.

PENAL CODE AND CRIMINAL INVESTIGATION TRUE AND FALSE TEST (Read Questions Well) 

1. T F Where in a homicide case the prisoner upon arrest denies all knowledge of the killing it can be safely assumed that he will not rely on the plea of self defense. 

2. T F Wrapping a revolver in a silk handkerchief is the best method of preserving fingerprints upon it. 

3. T F A wound which appears to have been made by a sharp instrument may, in fact, have been caused by a blow with a board. 

4. T F Evidence which may at first seem valueless may ultimately turn out to be of the highest value. 

5. T F More miscarriages of justice result from insufficient preperation and presentation of evidence than from any other cause. 

6. TFA confession which includes false statements cannot be used as evidence. 

7. TF The slightest promise of benefit to a prisoner will prevent his subsequent confession from being used in court.

13. T F A handwriting expert's success depends largely upon the amount of writing given to him to examine.

T F Stolen property found in a room occupied by one man alone is prima facie in his possession even though concealed. 

44. T F The crime of receiving stolen property is one of the most difficult crimes of which to convict an accused.

46. T. F An investigation of a homicide should be directed to finding the instrument causing death or any other means used to that end.

58. T F An officer as a witness testifies to his opinions, and the defendant testifies to facts.

62. T F An investigation officer must have no reasonable doubt as to the arrested person's guilt until he appears as a witness against the defendant. 

63. T F The appearance of an officer on the witness stand with notes on the case would bar him from acting as a witness. 

64. T F An officer knows immediately what portion of the available evidence at the scene of the crime will be important at the trial as evidence. 

65. T F It is part of the duty of the investigating officer to anticipate what evidence the defense might use at the trial. 

66. T F The lands and grooves of a revolver are important evidence when a gun is used in a murder. 

67. T F The land and groove marks on the lead of a discharged bullet will identify the make of gun used to fire the bullet. 

68. T F The trained and efficient investigating officer refrains from forming a definite theory at the outset of the investigation.

Heel prints have brought many criminals to justice who might have otherwise escaped punishment. Foot prints are too often overlooked by the investigating officer, because he does not understand modern methods of photographing and lifting the prints. After Before

HEEL PRINTS TIRE PRINTS 

After 

TRY THIS: 

Place a screen or card a little beyond the end of the print you are about to liftfirst take photograph, then spray print with alcohol and shellac, by spraying against the screen or card and let the mist fall on print to set before pouring the plaster of paris. This should bring good results. Moulage also may be used instead of plaster of paris, but proper preparation is necessary in any event. Remember there is a negative and positive on every print-the lifted impression being the positive.

69. T F A photo of the scene of a crime is always the best evidence an investigating officer can procure. 

70. T F A confession is the best evidence at all times to present to a jury to get a conviction. 

71. T F Free and voluntary confession means the defendant signed his name to the confession and therefore it is admissable in court. 

72. T F Corpus Delicti means the proof of guilt beyond a reasonable doubt. 

73. T F When two or more officers are detailed to an investigation they should work together in order to be able to cover the same subjects in their testimony. 

74. T F No confession can be received as evidence or even considered by the court unless there is independent evidence which standing alone, proves every element of the crime charged. 

75. T F Tire marks of the same tire are different when sliding forward, sideways, and backward. 

76. T F Footprints in soft dirt might tend to prove the height and weight of the criminal who committed the crime. 

77. T F The investigating officer should have a tool kit to fit his requirements and take same with him on all investigations.

The following points are important to note in an investigation of a homicide: objects moved dusty areas marks on wall, floor, ceiling windows, locks, carpets blood spots 87. T F Particular care should be devoted to doors and windows to see if they are locked or unlocked when making an investigation inside a building. 

88. T F Cigarette and cigar butts whether on the floor, in ash trays or a fire place, should not be overlooked.

92. T F The location and accurate description, should be recorded of every wound or injury, however slight, appearing on the body. 

93. T F The position of the body, location of customary objects, such as furniture, weapon, and location are worthy of the most careful attention. 

94. T F Before anything is touched by anyone at a homicide, photographs should be taken showing the position of the body, the wounds on the body, and any additional photographs which might by any possibility be of value in the future.

95. T F Pools of blood and size, shape, depth, location, and condition, as to whether wet or dry, congealed or coagulated, should be noted and the same applies to drops and spatters. 

96. T F If a homicide occurs indoors the means of entrance, walls and floors should be examined. 

97. T F The clothing of the deceased should be carefully examined noting the various garments, and color, condition of the pockets and their contents. 

98. T F If a homicide occurs indoors the means of entrance and exit should be noted with a view to determining how the pepetrator entered and departed. 

99. T F If any one thing is more important than any other in an investigation, it is that of visible marks connected with the crime. 

100. TF A person may become unconscious without having received any physical violence. 

101. TF A false statement by an accused person has little value as evidence. 

102. TF An admission by a defendant can be used as evidence against him even though it was not free and voluntary. 

103. TFA confession cannot be used against the defendant if made to an officer who had promised to help the prisoner get probation. 

104. TF A bullet has no value as evidence if the rifling marks have been obliterated. 

105. TF A witness can use a written statement he previously made to refresh his memory on the witness stand. 

106. TF One bullet cannot cause more than two holes in the person of the victim. 

107. TF Science can identify blood as coming from a human being. 

108. TF Blood spots are always red or brownish in color. 

109. TF No other substance will make a spot that looks like blood. 

110. TF No special skill is required to photograph automobile tire tracks. 

111. TF A police officer can never testify as an expert witness. 

112. TF The person who reports the crime or makes the complaint may be the guilty person.

113. TF Science can tell whether two separate samples of paint are identical or not. 

114. TF The dying declaration of a person who has met his death by criminal means can only be used as evidence in a homicide case. 

115. TF A defendant can never use as evidence the dying declaration of the person he is charged with killing. 

116. TF If a body is found with a knife stuck through the heart this is conclusive evidence that he was killed by stabbing.

Vehicle Code Test 

1. T F (238) License plates registered to one car may be removed and placed upon another car and not be in violation of the 

Vehicle Code. 

2. T F (252) For one year, if over twenty-one years of age, a nonresident may operate his car under his home State license. 

3. T F (254) Temporary licenses are issued which allow anyone to operate a motor vehicle for a period of not over sixty (60) days, if the person has successfully passed such test as is required by the Motor Vehicle Department. 

4. T F (257) A minor between the ages of fourteen (14) and sixteen (16) years cannot be issued any kind of an operator's license. 

5. T F (350) According to the Vehicle Code, all persons under the age of twenty-one (21) years shall be deemed minors. 

6. T F (255) A plain operator's license will never allow anyone to drive as a chauffeur over a period of twenty-four hours. 

7. T F (83) A roadway is that portion of a highway improved, designed or ordinarily used for vehicular travel. 

8. T F (253) An unlicensed person learning to drive, while in the company of a licensed operator need have no license or permit. 

9. T F (250a) The driver of an automobile or motorcycle on a race track does not need an operator's license. 

10. T F (256) A licensed chauffeur must have, in addition to his chauffeur's license, a regular operator's license. 

11. T F (71) One could not be a chauffeur legally and not receive compensation therefor. 

12. T F (310) Non-residents are exempt from most of the rules set forth in the California Vehicle Code. 

13. T F (313) A suspension of license usually means not more than six months. 

14. T F (252) A non-resident chauffeur, of twenty-one years or over, may not operate under a foreign chauffeur's license if he is accepting employment from a resident of this State. 

15. T F (338a) It is unlawful for any person to display or represent as one's own any operator's license or chauffeur's license not issued to said person. 

16. T F (332) When a person's operator's or chauffeur's license has been revoked and he is found to be driving a vehicle, the penalty could be $1000 fine, but not a jail sentence.

17. T F (258-259) J, a high school student aged sixteen (16), has a valid chauffeur's license. He drives the school bus night and morning; he is not violating the Vehicle Code. 

18. T F (220) There are certain police reports of stolen and recovered vehicles that must be filed with the State Motor Vehicle Department. 

19. T F (68) The "legal owner" is always considered to be the registered owner of a vehicle. 

20. T F (130) All records of the Motor Vehicle Department relat ing to the registration of vehicles and licenses of operators or chauffeurs shall be open to public inspection during office hours. 

21. T F (89) A business district is where, for a distance of 600 feet on one side of the street or highway, 50% or more of the contiguous property fronting thereto is occupied by business houses; or where, for the distance of 300 feet on both sides, collectively, of the street or highway, 50% or more of the contiguous property fronting thereto is occupied by business. 

22. T F (251a) A person in the naval or military service of the 

United States and acting as a chauffeur in such service, nevertheless, is required to obtain a chauffeur's license. 

23. T F (334) E, an employer, cannot be held responsible if he hires a chauffeur and it is found said chauffeur has no valid chauffeur's license. 

24. T F (277) All chauffeur's licenses issued from this date shall expire two years from date issued. 

25. T F (276) All operator's licenses issued from this date shall expire four years from date issued.

Penal Code Questions TRUE AND FALSE 

Directions: The following questions are either true or false. If the statement is true, encircle the "T," if false, encircle the "F." Any left unmarked will be counted as incorrect. 

(Superseded by Elections Code) 

1. T F A, a voter, watches the election board count the ballots. To assist the board, A unfolds a pile of the ballots. A is not guilty of any crime, unless he had an intent to defraud. (Sec. 40.) 

2. T F H, a clerk at an election, put a mark on V's ballot, to see how 

V voted. He is guilty of a felony. (Sec. 49). 

3. T F V demanded the right to vote and requested J, election judge, to swear him and take evidence. J refused, and referred V to the registrar of voters to determine his right to vote. As there was no fraudulent intent, J is not guilty of any crime. (Sec. 41.) 

4. TF R, a deputy registrar, registers X to vote, with knowledge that X is twenty years old. R is guilty of a felony and so is X. (Sec. 42 and 42a.) 

5. T F A, a registered voter, votes at an election. Several hours later, A attempts to get a ballot, but is refused. Honest mistake on A's part and lack of intent to defraud will not be a good defense. (Sec. 46.) 

6. TFA has registered as a voter, giving his party affiliation as a 

Communist. A week before the election, B tells A he had better not attempt to vote. A nevertheless votes. B is guilty of a felony. (Sec. 53.) 

7. T F On the eve of a national election, A bet B $10 that Pennsylvania will go Republican. As this bet does not concern an election in California, it is no crime. (Sec. 60.) 

8. T F On the eve of an election in Canada, A bet B $10 that Ontario would vote against racing. A is not guilty of any crime. (Sec. 60.) 

9. T F E tells his employees that if X is elected, he is sure business conditions will get worse. E is guilty of a misdemeanor. (Sec. 59.)

10. T F Y is on trial for falsely registering as a voter. R, who registered Y, is called by Y as a witness. R refuses on the ground that it will incriminate him, but the court compels him to testify. R can now claim immunity from being prosecuted for falsely registering Y. (Sec. 64.) 

11. T F Officer O has a search warrant to search X's premises for stolen goods. X blockades the way, barricades the door and threatens to ignite a keg of gunpowder if O approaches. This is only a misdemeanor. (Secs. 69, 102 and 148.) 

12. T F In the above case, O seizes stolen goods found on X's premises. Y secretly takes the goods out of O's police car. Y can only be charged with theft. (Secs. 69, 102 and 148.) 

13. TFA submits evidence to city prosecutor C to establish that B has embezzled money while in A's employ. C tells A he will have to hire him to work on his own time auditing A's books. 

This is no offense. (Sec. 70.) 

14. TFA is a county supervisor, and owns stock in P's printing shop. A votes with the other supervisors to contract with

P to do the county printing. There was no other establishment in the county. A is guilty of a serious offense. (71.) 

15. T F In the above case A declines to vote and the contract is made by the other supervisors. A is guilty of a serious offense. (Sec. 71.) 

16. T F In the above case, A transferred his stock to his nephew 

N on advice of his attorney. This will be a good defense. 

(Sec. 71.) 

17. T F C, city purchaser had the authority to buy city supplies.

X falsely presented a written claim that he had delivered supplies to C. The treasurer T knew this was false and refused to pay X. As T knew the falsity of the claim, X could only be prosecuted for an attempt to commit theft and for no other offense. (Sec. 72.) 

18. T F J, judge, accepted $5.00 a month from O, his clerk, out of 

O's salary to be used for J's re-election. This is no crime. (Sec. 74a.) 

19. T F Mayor P received $100 for appointing X police commissioner. This is no crime. (Sec. 74.) 

20. T F S offered to give J, judge, half his fees if J appointed S as shorthand reporter. S can never serve as a shorthand reporter in any California court. (Sec. 94.)

21. 

T F Senator S is interested in the passage of a certain bill. Assemblyman A is interested in another measure. S tells A he will vote for A's bill and asks A to study S's bill. This is not a crime. (Sec. 86.)

T F P, convicted of petty theft, is serving his sentence in the city jail. J, jailor, sends P to the corner to get J a package of cigarettes. P runs away. Both P and J are guilty of a felony. (Secs. 107 and 108.)

TFA was likely to be summoned as a witness in a case not yet filed in Court. B influenced A to leave town until the case was over. B has not committed a felony. (Sec. 136.) 

25. TF In the above case, C offered W $100 if he would remain in town and testify. C has not committed a felony. (Secs. 

136 to 138.)

27. TF In the above case, D tells X, a witness, that he will give X $100 to stay away from the trial. D is guilty of a felony. (Sec. 1362.) 

28. T F B, a citizen, arrested C for petty theft committed in B's presence. B ordered Officer O to take C into custody. O refuses, as O was not present when C committed the theft. 

In wilfully refusing to take into custody persons arrested by a citizen upon his demand, an officer is guilty of a misdemeanor in all cases. (Sec. 142.) 

29. T F B, citizen, arrests C for petty theft and has him booked at the police station. Court was not in session. C made bail after one hour in jail. B did not secure a complaint. B is guilty of a misdemeanor. (Sec. 145.) 

30. TF Officer O arrests T on X's complaint that T committed battery on X. The battery was committed in O's presence. X refuses later to file a complaint against T. X is guilty of a misdemeanor. (Sec. 145.)

146 and 236.)

O approaches to arrest him. W is guilty of a crime, in spite of the fact that the gun was empty. (Sec. 417.)

Criminal Investigation Examination 

Directions: Most of the following questions are either true or false. If the statement is true, encircle the "T," if false, encircle the "F."

In other questions, special directions are given to mark by V or X. Follow directions on these questions. Any left unmarked will be counted as incorrect. 

1. T F The mere fact that a revolver is found in the possession of the accused is not ordinarily alone sufficient to permit its introduction at the trial. 

2. TFA dying declaration can only be used as evidence in a homicide case. 

3. TF Paint from a window frame may be found on a jimmy and an expert can identify the paint. 

4. T F Excitement or nervousness necessarily interferes with or prevents an accurate and positive observation. 

5. T F There is no rule or standard by which we can measure a witness in advance as to whether he will be able to make a reliable identification. 

6. T F It is a natural tendency for the investigating officer to desire an identification of the person whom he believes to be the guilty party. 

7. T F If the person making a dying declaration really believes he is dying the declaration may be used as evidence if he recovers.

Check the following V is true; X if false.

In securing the witness' statement the investigator should seek particularly for identifying details: manner of speech features religion nationality 

10. T F A mole or scar may be faked. scars moles deformities type of voice 

11. T F Witnesses should be promptly interviewed before they have a chance to get together to talk it over.

12. T F Where a suspect is under arrest it is an extremely good practice to bring him before the witness by himself for identification. 

13. T F A criminal should be appraised of the fact immediately upon his identification by a witness. 

14. TF Great care and caution must be exercised in recording and checking the testimony of eye witness. 15. 

Check the following V if true; X if false.

The following are likely to produce inaccurate, exaggerated or biased statements: excitement fear anger emotions influence bias favor caution 16. T F The law of self defense is predicated upon the existence of an actual or apparent necessity for killing. 

17. T F A diagram of the scene of a homicide, accurately depicting anything which by any stretch of imagination could be of any significance, should be prepared as soon as possible. 

18. TF Reenactment of an affray to check the stories of the witnesss would make the evidence inadmissable in court. 

19. T F A dying declaration being hearsay evidence cannot be received in court. 

20. T F "Under a sense of impending death" means that the declarant must at the time of making the statement believe he will die as the result of his injuries. 

21. T F The obvious cause of death may not be the real cause. 

22. T F Stab wounds are sometimes smaller than the width of the blade causing them. 

23. T F Gunshot wounds at short range often have particles of clothing carried into the wound. 

24. T F All wounds caused by pistol or rifle bullets do not necessarily come from weapons of that class. 

25. T F You can tell by chemical test whether a spot is human blood or not. 

26. T F Even though a person whose hands have been bloody washes them to the point where he believes that all traces of blood have been removed, there is still an excellent chance of finding blood under the fingernails, between the cuticle and nail or in creases in the skin to enable the laboratory expert to identify it.

27. T F The value of fingerprints as evidence depends upon the type of homicide. 

28. T F The cost of materials for taking fingerprints is so expensive and so hard to use that only the larger departments should try to take fingerprints at the scene of a crime.

30. T F It is useless to look for fingerprints on paper.

Footprints can show if a person is lame has short leg is pigeon toed walks with feet apart walks with feet close has long or short stride 33. T F The first step in preserving footprint evidence should be to photograph the print as a record with a ruler laid beside the print.

39. T F Evidence of experiments conducted to prove or disprove the statements of the defendant or any of the witnesses is often a valuable means of arriving at the truth. 

40. TF Evidence of experiments cannot be used unless the experiments are made under absolutly identical conditions.

Written Discussion Questions 

The correct answers to the following questions are brief and concise. 

Only such answers will count. 

1. State briefly the property generally exempt from execution or attachment. When do these exemptions not apply? 

2. What are the qualities most required in a peace officer? 

3. Into what classes are crimes classified, and how may they be distinguished? 

4. For a peace officer, what is the distinction between a civil and a criminal action? 

5. An officer unlawfully enters a house to make a search without a warrant. While he is thus unlawfully in the house, a misdemeanor is committed in his presence. Has he the right to make an arrest for the misdemeanor? 

6. As a peace officer would you be guilty of any crime if you refused to make the arrest of a person who has committed a felony but who is obviously physically superior to you and thoroughly armed? If so, what crime? (Answers on page 123)

7. A defendant held up three people in a group and took wallets and jewelry from each of the three. May he be charged with one or three robberies? 

8. An officer can make an arrest without a warrant when he has reasonable grounds for believing that a felony is about to be committed; a private citizen cannot. But is this always true? If not, can you think of a circumstance where a citizen could make a lawful arrest under these conditions? 

9. Besides warrants of arrest, what two other familiar processes are issued to require attendance in court, and is serving them the same as making an arrest? 

10. What is meant by extradition? Is it true that it applies only in felony cases? 

11. If a player in a football game wilfully kicks another player, is this a crime, and if so, what? 

12. Name the courts of record. Name the inferior courts.

Penal Code Questions TRUE AND FALSE 

If Statement True Circle "T" If False, Circle "F" 1. T F A crime is an act committed or omitted in violation of a law forbidding or commanding it to be done and for which there is provided a punishment. 

2. T F In every crime, the state must establish the joint union of intent and motive. 3. 

T F Crimes are divided into felonies and misdemeanors. 

4. TF Unless the offense is punishable by death or imprisonment in State prison it is not a felony. 

5. T F Where a crime is made punishable by imprisonment in State prison or county jail or by fine, it is prosecuted as a felony. 

6. T F Where, in the above case, the court sentences the defendant, on conviction, to the county jail, it is still a felony conviction. 

7. T F A is convicted of assault, the punishment for which is a fine not exceeding $500 or by imprisonment in the county jail not exceeding six months or by both. In sentencing A, the court must fix the exact amount of fine to be paid. 

8. T F B is convicted of assault with a deadly weapon, punishable by imprisonment in State prison not exceeding ten years, or in the county jail not exceeding one year or by fine not exceeding $5000 or by both such fine and imprisonment. The court sentences B to pay a fine of $500 and to serve two years in State prison. The court is right. 

9. TFA unlawfully hits B with a club. B dies from the injury.

A is presumed by law to have intended to kill B. 

10. T F X unlawfully hits Y with a club. Y recovers from the injury. X is charged with an assault with intent to murder 

Y. The law does not presume that X intends to murder Y, but the State will have to prove by facts that X had the intent to murder Y. 

11. T F Voluntary intoxication is not a good defense in any case. 

12. T F Minors under the age of seven are not criminally responsible for their acts. 

13. T F Minors under 14 are responsible unless they can prove beyond a reasonable doubt that they did not know the wrongfulness of their acts.

14. T F Idiots are not criminally responsible for their acts in any case. 

15. T F An idiot is one whose brain development is only that of a child of about two years. 

16. T F Ignorance of the law does not excuse anyone. 

17. T F Mistake or ignorance of fact will always excuse one. 

18. T F D is charged with rape upon F, aged 16. She told D she was 

21. Ignorance of her true age is a defense for D. 

19. T F Acts committed by misfortune and accident are always excusable. 

20. TFA and B are fighting. A, intending to strike B, starts a heavy blow, but strikes X who has stepped in the way.

Because it was by misfortune and accident, A is not guilty of an assault on X. 

21. T F Where a wife commits any crime because of the threats and coercion of her husband, she is not criminally liable. 

22. TFA threatens to kill B unless B kills C. Afraid of his life,

B thus kills C. No crime has been committed by B, because he acted to save his own life. 

23. TF X threatens to kill Y on the spot unless Y robs Z. Afraid of his life, Y robs Z. No crime has been committed by Y, because he acted to save his own life. 

24. T F Intending to kill B, in New York, X, living in Los Angeles, prepares and sends B a box of poisoned candy. X can only be tried for B's death in New York. 

25. TF In Arizona, Y robs Z, and brings the stolen loot into Los 

Angeles. Y may be prosecuted in Los Angeles. 

26. T F In Arizona, A stabs B who dies the next day in Los Angeles, where A is caught. A may be tried for murder in Los Angeles. 

27. T F The parties to crime are principals and accessories. 

28. T F A, in Riverside, committed a robbery and then came to Los 

Angeles where he told B what he had done. B conceals A, and assists him to escape. A and B are principals. 

29. T F Treason cannot be committed by a foreigner. 

30. T F Police officers are executive officers. 

31. T F P, officer, arrests C, city councilman, for a traffic violation. 

C offers to vote for P to get the appointment of chief of police, if P will not give him a citation. C is definitely guilty of bribery. 

32. T F Extortion is the obtaining of any property of another, with his consent, induced by a wrongful use of force or fear.

33. T F Robbery is the felonious taking of personal property of another against his will, from his person or immediate presence accomplished by force or fear. 

34. T F Both extortion and robbery are felonies without proof of any special amount of value of the property wrongfully taken. 

35. TF Grand larceny, a felony, may be established where the personal property is stolen from the person or immediate presense of another, without proof of any special amount of value. 

36. T F Murder is the unlawful killing of a human being with malice aforethought. 

37. T F Malice is of two kinds-expressed and implied. 

38. TF Unless the unlawful killing was by means of poison, torture or lying in wait, or in the perpetraton of rape, robbery, burglary, arson or mayhem, it is second degree murder. 

39. T F Manslaughter is an unlawful killing without malice. 

40. TF Where the killing is on a sudden quarrel and heat of passion, it is expressed manslaughter. 41. 

T F Where the killing is in the doing of an unlawful act, not amounting to felony, or in the doing of a lawful act, which might produce death, in an unlawful manner, it is called implied manslaughter. 

42. T F Where the evidence clearly shows the killing was excusable, the defendant can be convicted of manslaughter only. 

43. T F Justifiable homicide is not a crime. 

44. TFA peace officer, but not a citizen, can kill another in attempting to arrest another in the commission of a misdemeanor. 

45. T F If one maliciously bites another's ear, it is mayhem. 

46. T F If one maliciously cuts another's lip or nose, but does not slit it, it is not mayhem. 47. 

T F Arson is the malicious burning of a dwelling or other building connected with the dwelling, whether it is solely owned and occupied by the defendants or by others. 48. 

T F The malicious burning of a warehouse is not arson. 

49. T F To prove burglary, there must be a breaking and entering of a building or room of another with intent to commit some felony or petty theft. 

50. T F The crime of burglary is not complete until some felony or petty theft is committed after the entrance is effected.

51. T F All burglaries committed in the night time, are burglaries of the first degree. 

52. T F The phrase "night time" is that period between dark and dawn. 

53. T F Where one in the perpetration of burglary, is armed with a deadly or dangerous weapon, it is burglary in the first degree. 

54. T F Where one, in the perpetration of robbery, is armed with a deadly or dangerous weapon, it is robbery in the first degree. 

55. T F The stealing of a dog is grand theft. 

56. T F Domestic fowls must be of a value of over fifty dollars to be the subject of grand theft. 

57. T F Stealing a pig worth five dollars is grand theft. 

58. T F Stealing avocados or citrus fruit worth $200 is only petty theft. 

59. TF A, by the use of a "jumper" on his meter, steals one dollar's worth of electricity each day for 250 consecutive days. A can be convicted of grand theft. 

60. TFA intrusts B with his watch to repair. B sells A's watch. 

B is guilty of larceny. 

61. T F Any person over 21 years of age may serve a warrant of arrest. 

62. T F A warrant of arrest for felony must be served within three years. 

63. TFA warrant of arrest for a misdemeanor must be served within one year. 

64. T F No warrant of arrest can be served at night unless indorsed by a magistrate. 65. 

TFA search warrant must be served within two weeks. 

66. T F Except peace officers, no person can make an arrest, without a warrant for any offense not committed in his presence. 

67. T F Officer O, without a warrant arrests X. After five days, O obtains a complaint, and takes X out of jail to the court. O is guilty of false imprisonment, although X is guilty of the charge for which he was arrested. 

68. TFA magistrate is any judge having the power to issue a warrant of arrest. 

69. T F Habeas corpus is the proceeding to determine whether or not a person is unlawfully restrained of his liberty. 

70. T F Corpus delicti is a judicial order to produce the body.

71. T F Arson of an inhabited dwelling in the night time is arson in the first degree. 

72. T F In the crimes of conspiracy, unlawful assembly, riot, rout and lynching, there is required the participation of two or more persons. 

73. T F In felony cases, the trial must be had with a jury. 

74. T F A grand jury is composed of twelve persons. 

75. T F A jury in a felony trial is composed of twelve persons. 

76. T F In misdemeanor trials, the jury may consist of only one person or any other number not more than twelve. 

77. T F To obtain a jury of inquest, there must be summoned not less than nine nor more than fifteen persons. When six or more appear, they must be sworn. 

78. T F On a plea of "not guilty by reason of insanity" the burden is on the defendant to prove he was insane, by the prepondderance of the evidence but not beyond a reasonable doubt. 

79. T F On a plea of "not guilty by reason of insanity," it is part of a criminal trial, and all the jurors must unanimously agree, in order to return a verdict. 

80. T F In civil trials, three-fourths of the jury may return a verdict. 

81. T F To return an indictment all of the grand jurors must vote. 

82. T F An indictment is the accusation in writing returned by the grand jury, charging the accused with the commission of a crime. 

83. T F A felony charge may be begun by obtaining a warrant of arrest from a magistrate based on complaint made before him, and after the defendant is arraigned before the magistrate, holding a preliminary hearing. 

84. T F A defendant may waive a preliminary hearing and be held to answer for trial in the Superior Court. 

85. T F Admission to bail is the order of a competent court that the defendant be discharged from actual custody upon bail. 

86. T F A defendant charged with an offense which is punishable by death cannot be admitted to bail, even where the proof of his guilt is very weak. 

87. T F The crimes of embezzlement of public money, falsification of public records and murder never outlaw. 

88. T F The prosecution of all other felonies except those stated in 

87, must be initiated in the filing of an indictment or information within three years. 

89. T F The statute of limitations in all misdemeanor cases is one year.

90. T F A crime can be proved solely by a confession. 

91. T F Corpus delicti means the body of a dead person. 

92. T F It requires three or more persons to commit a riot. 

93. T F The law requires that a dying declaration be in writing. 

94 T F No overt act is required to be proved in a prosecution of conspiracy. 

95. TFA hearing held by the coroner to have a jury determine the cause of death of a person is called a post mortem. 

96. T F Criminology means the study of crime. 

97. T F Penology means the study of punishment. 

98. T F In no case can a female be guilty of rape. 

99. T F If an uncle-in-law marries his niece-in-law, the crime of incest has been committed. 

100. TF Officer O, to get evidence on J, pretends to assist J in committing a theft. This activity on O's part is known as enticement. 

101. TF To commit burglary, it is necessary that force be used to obtain an entrance. 

102. TF If a wound is unlawfully inflicted, resulting in death, the fact life could have been saved by proper medical aid which the physician in charge would have been able to give, constitutes a defense in felonious homicide. 

103. TF After making an arrest, a peace officer's first duty should be to obtain the person's name, address, age and nationality. 

104. TF An officer can make an arrest in a civil action without a warrant. 

105. TF All furniture, merchandise, chattels, etc., which can be moved are said to be personal property capable of manual delivery. 

106. TF Grand juries are selected by a vote of the people in large counties. 

107. TF In order for a dying statement to be admissible, it is necessary that the person making such statement died immediately afterwards. 

108. TF A killing in necessary self defense is called excusable homicide. 

109. TFA search warrant may legally be issued without probable cause that circumstances that warrant such issuance exist. 

110. TF Persons riding bicycles or other non-motorized vehicles do not come under the regulations of the Vehicle Code. 

111. TF To establish a conspiracy there must be proof of an overt act.

112.

TF No person may assume a fictitious name without a court order. 

113. TF Punishment by imprisonment in the penitentiary is only given in felony cases. 

114. TF The court by the sentence imposed may determine whether certain crimes are misdemeanors or felonies. 

115. TF California does not recognize any distinction between an accessory before the fact and a principal.

Written Description Test 

Report of Officers Pat Murphy and James O'Brien,

Precinct No. 30, Shift No. 2, Radio Car W-106. 

While proceeding South on Green Blvd. 4 miles east of the city, a mile and a half west of Hidden Lake, we came on a personal injury accident at the intersection of Green Blvd. and Fells Lane. On investigation we found that a pedestrian had been struck by one of the trolley cars of the Interurban Co. traveling from Ross to Wells. 

On our arrival the pedestrian was lying 16 feet in front of the car and was unconscious. Officer O'Brien made a brief examination, opened the victim's slicker and suit coat, and ascertained that his heart was still beating. There apparently were no broken bones. 

From letters in his pocket the victim was identified as Mr. Henry 

Tew of Lawton. He was approximately 5 feet 10 inches tall, 45 years of age, 165 pounds, dark complexioned, brown eyes and gray hair. It is apparent that he had been struck with some force since he had been thrown 16 feet ahead of the car and his umbrella had been thrown to the curb 20 feet away by the impact. His expensive Waltham watch had stopped at 10:02, which we assume to be the time of the accident. 

On our arrival there was a group of persons gathered. The motorman and conductor claim that they were unable to see the victim before the impact. Mrs. John Harris of Chelsea, who was driving behind the street car, was an eye witness and gave information as to the cause of the accident. Mr. Reg Groon of Sopona, who was riding with her, corroborated her statements. Mr. Tom Clint, who was walking close to the accident, verified their statements. He further stated that he had phoned Dr. Lloyd Tay and that the doctor was on his way with an ambulance.

There were three autos parked at the curb belonging to the bystanders. These bore license numbers as follows: New Mexico 19468; 

New York 3N1716; California 50Y1493. By the light of the arc lamps we were able to distinguish the sign post which gave us the exact mileage to the south as Sopona 3½ miles, Wells 6 miles. We estimated that the doctor should arrive from Wells within 10 minutes. This proved to be correct and within 10 minutes he arrived with an ambulance from the Ruffin Emergency Hospital and took the patient away for treatment. (Turn to Page 130 for Questions)

Test Your Knowledge of the United States Constitution ALSO GENERAL INFORMATION QUESTIONS AND TRUE AND FALSE TEST 

1. T F The Constitution was established by authority of the thirteen original states. 

2. T F The establishment of justice is one of the expressed purposes of the Constitution. 

3. T F Establishment of police and military forces is implied in the 

Preamble. 

4.

TF Religious freedom is one of the purposes enumerated in the 

Preamble. 

5.

T F The Preamble definitely identifies the Union as the United 

States of America. 

6. T F More perfect national unity is one of the objects expressly declared in the Preamble. 7. 

T F Promotion of the general welfare is also specifically mentioned. 

8. T F The word "freedom" appears in the Preamble. 

9. TF The Preamble specifically states that this Constitution shall become the "law of the land." 

10. T F Domestic tranquility is mentioned as one of the objects of the Constitution. 

11. T F All persons born or naturalized in the United States and subject to its jurisdiction are citizens of the United States and of the State wherein they reside. 

12. T F Private property may be taken for public use, when circumstances demand it, without compensation to the property owner. 

13. T F Under a certain definite condition, involuntary servitude may still exist in the United States. 

14. T F An official of the United States Government, may at his option receive a title of nobility from a foreign government. 

15. T F The right of citizens of any state to vote cannot be abridged or denied on account of sex, race, color, or previous condition of servitude. 

16. T F No person may be punished for breaking a law established after the alleged offense occurred.

17. T F In a criminal case, a person may be required to be a witness against himself. 

18. TF In civil suits, where the value in controversy shall exceed $20, the defendant is entitled to the right of trial by jury. 

19. T F The only grounds of treason against the United States are waging war against it, or assisting its enemies. 

20. T F Powers not specifically delegated to the States by the Constitution are reserved to the federal government to be delegated as Congress may see fit.

The President: Duties, Rights, Powers and Restrictions 

21. T F It is the duty of the President of the United States to see that all laws passed by Congress and either signed by him or passed over his veto are faithfully executed. 

22. T F The President is Commander-in-Chief of all military forces of the United States, including the militia of the several states. 

23. TF By act of Congress, the salary of the President can be increased or diminished during his term of office. 

24. T F In vetoing a bill passed by the House of Representatives and the Senate, the President must state his objections to the legislation. 

25. TF In case of impeachment and conviction of a cabinet officer, the President could grant a pardon and restore the offender to office. 

26. T F In making treaties, the President is specifically directed to do so by and with the advice and consent of the Senate. 

27. TF Appointment by the President of ambassadors, consuls, judges of the Supreme Court, and other officers of the United States, must be approved by the Senate before they can become effective. 

28. T F The President may, if conditions warrant, call a special session of both Houses of Congress or either one of them alone. 

29. T F The President has the right to make recommendations to

Congress regarding legislation that he believes is necessary and expedient. 

30. T F If, five days before they adjourn, the Senate and the House, pass a bill and the President does not sign it, the bill automatically becomes law.

The Questions and Answers and the numerous Tests contained herein, were edited and compiled for the enlightenment of the Peace Officers for Home 

Study, by A. Beldon Gilbert, Executive Director, Peace Officers Civil Serv ice Association of California.

Courts: Rights, Powers and Restrictions 

31. T F The right to establish more United States courts is vested in the Supreme Court. 

32. T F In the event of impeachment of the President, the Chief

Justice of the Supreme Court presides over the Senate when the impeachment is tried. 

33. T F Trial of all crime, except cases of impeachment, shall be by jury. 

34. T F If it is suspected that a dangerous criminal may be in hiding in a certain town, a United States court may issue a general search warrant authorizing officers of the law to search at their discretion, any house in that town. 

35. TF Indictment by a grand jury is necessary for trial for capital crimes, except in the military services. 

36. T F A person tried for a crime and acquitted may be tried again at some later date if new evidence is discovered. 

37. T F The same compulsion by law shall be applied to obtaining witnesses in favor of an accused person as is used to compel the attendance of witnesses for the prosecution. 

38. T F In controversies between citizens of different states, the 

United States has jurisdicton. 

39. TF Criminal prosecution shall be held in the state and district wherein the crime has been committed. 

40. T F The testimony of a single reputable person is sufficient to convict a person accused of treason before a United States court.

Congress; Duties, Rights and Powers 

41. T F Congress has the power to lay and collect taxes on income without apportionment among the several states and without regard to any census or enumeration. 

42. T F Congress has the power to call out the militia of the various states. 

43. TF Congress, when it thinks proper, may delegate the appointment of inferior government officers to the President alone. 

44. TFA motion for impeachment of the President, Vice-President, or any civil officer of the United States must arise in the 

House of Representatives. 

45. TF Impeachments are tried by the House of Representatives and the Senate in joint session. 

46. TFA bill vetoed by the President must first be reconsidered by the House of Congress in which it originated.

47. T F A vote of three-quarters of both Houses of Congress is required to pass a bill over a Presidential veto. 

48. T F The Constitution specifies that in the event of death of both 

President and Vice-President, the Secretary of State shall become President. 

49. T F Congress has the power to enforce, within a state, the provisions that uphold the rights of all citizens to vote, regardless of sex, race, color, or previous condition of servitude. 

50. T F Congress may, at its discretion, impose export taxes on goods shipped from one state to another, in order to regulate the flow of commerce among the states. 

States; Rights, Powers and Restrictions 

51. T F The supreme law of the land consists of the Constitution, the laws of the United States that conform to the Constitution, and treaties made under the authority of the United 

States. 

52. T F Members of state legislatures and all executive and judicial officers of each state are required to take an oath to support the Constitution of the United States. 

53. T F It is possible for a person who has been guilty of treason to serve as a member of Congress. 

54. T F The transportation or importation of liquor into any state whose laws prohibit. it is expressly an offense against the 

United States. 

55. TF In order for one state to enter into any agreement or compact with another state, it is necessary to obtain the consent of Congress. 

56. T F Application by the legislature of three-fourths of the states is necessary to propose an amendment to the Constitution. 

57. T F The Constitution makes it mandatory for governors of the various states to fill vacancies in the House and Senate by appointment, pending the election of a new Senator or Representative in the next regular election. 

58. T F The electoral vote of any state for the election of President and Vice-President is equal to the total number of Senators and Representatives to which that state is entitled. 

59. TF Under certain conditions, the militia of a state may legally engage in war without a declaration of war by Congress, or orders from the President. 

60. TF No tax or duty shall be laid on articles exported from any state.

QUALIFICATIONS FOR OFFICE 

61. T F The Constitution makes it illegal for a Mohammedan to become a United States Senator. 

62. T F A person born in England, and having been brought to the 

United States by his parents six weeks after birth, would become eligible to the Presidency after reaching his thirtyfifth birthday. 

63. T F No one but a natural-born citizen of the United States is eligible for the office of Vice-President. 

64. T F No one less than 30 years of age can become a U. S. Senator. 

65. T F Anyone more than 21 years of age, who has been a citizen of the United States for more than ten years, may be elected to the House of Representatives. 

66. T F In case of a tie in the electoral vote for President, the President shall be chosen by the House of Representatives. 

67. T F The term of office of a U. S. Senator is four years. 

68. T F The term of office of a Representative is two years. 

69. T F In the event of death of both the President and Vice-President, Congress will decide what officer shall then act as President until a new President is elected. 

70. T F Upon certain election dates, it would be possible to have a complete change of all members of the Senate. 

Procedure of Legislative, Executive and Judicial Departments 71. T F The date of inauguration of a new President and Vice- 

President is the 20th day of January. 

72. T F Congress is required to assemble once each year. 

73. T F Either the House or the Senate may, by two-thirds vote, expel one of its members. 

74. T F The Speaker of the House of Representatives shall be the senior member of that body. 

75. T F Neither house, during a session of Congress, may adjourn for more than three days without mutual consent of both houses. 

76. T F All bills for raising revenue originate in the Senate. 

77. T F The Constitution requires that a census be made every ten years. 

78. T F The quorum required for the transaction of any business by either the Senate or the House consists of a majority of the membership.

79. T F In the event of disagreement between the Senate and the 

House regarding time of adjournment, the President of the United States may adjourn them to such time as he shall think proper. 

80. T F A record of the vote of the members of either house of Congress must be made if one-fifth of those present desire it. 

Amendments 

81.

T F The first ten amendments to the Constitution constitute the 

Bill of Rights. 

82. T F The Fifteenth Amendment to the Constitution gave equal rights to white and colored citizens. 

83. T F The Twelfth Amendment abolished slavery. 

TF The Fourteenth Amendment made invalid any state law that abridged privileges or immunities of citizens of the 

United States. 

85. T F The levying of income taxes by the United States was not legal until made so by the Sixteenth Amendment. 

86. T F Until ratification of the Seventeenth Amendment, Senators were elected by the state legislatures. 

87. T F The Eighteenth Amendment was made invalid by the adoption of the Twenty-first. 

88. T F Prior to 1920, women could not vote for President, Vice- 

President, or members of Congress. 

89. T F The Twentieth Amendment changed the term of office of 

U. S. Senators from four to six years. 

90. T F The two amendments that most greatly increased the number of voters in the United States were the 13th and the 19th. General 

91. T F Congress has the sole power to declare war. 

92. T F It is within the power of Congress to lay down a ten-year plan for the development of the national defense and to appropriate the money to raise and support the military organization necessary for carrying out this plan. 

93. T F The President of the United States is elected by direct majority vote of the people. 

94. T F Any state Constitution or state law violating any of the provisions of the federal Constitution is illegal. 

95. TF The subject of education is not mentioned any place in the Constitution.

96. T F A member of the President's cabinet could be, at the same time, a Senator or a Representative. 

97. T F The original Constitution provided for the formation of the 

President's cabinet, exactly as now constituted. 

98. T F The Secretary of State, acting by authority of the President, may recognize a foreign government without the consent of Congress. 

99. T F A resolution by Congress proposing an Amendment to the 

Constitution must be approved by the President before it goes to the states to be ratified. 

100. TF An amendment to the Constitution can be repealed only by adding another amendment for that purpose.

Vehicle Code Test 1. T F (163) Under no circumstances may the Division of Motor 

Vehicles refuse to renew a vehicle registraton just because the registered owner has not complied with a Police Court order. 

2. T F (231) Every person holding a valid driver's license must notify the Division of Motor Vehicles of a change of address within fifteen days after such change is effected. 

3. TF (274) A court must dismiss a charge of failure to have a driver's license if at the time of appearance, the defendant produces a license that was valid at the time of arrest. 

4. T F (292) The court must suspend the driver's license where the driver is convicted of reckless driving if bodily injury is caused by such reckless driving. 

5. TF (454) The driver of an emergency vehicle is exempt from most provisions of the Vehicle Code in all cases. 

6. TF (484) The driver of a common carrier does not have to make an accident report to the police department if the accident causes bodily injury or death. 

7. T F (505) Reckless driving is an unlawful disregard for the safety of persons or property. 

8. T F (510) Under the new code, our only speed regulation is the basic speed law. 

9. TF (515.5) Vehicles drawing trailer coaches are now restricted to a maximum of 35 miles per hour. 

10. T F (516) The speed on bridges is specific as to both the maximum and minimum that is allowed. 

11. T F (607.1) It is perfectly legal to charge a vehicle for the use of a vehicular crossing. 

12. T F (618) For the purpose of the Vehicle Code, lights are required on all vehicles between sunset and sunrise. 

13. T F (632) Acetylene headlights are not legal under the Vehicle

Code of 1937.

14. T F (736) A person arrested for a violation of the Vehicle Code must be taken before a magistrate at once if he cannot show a valid driver's license or other satisfactory identification. 

15. T F (315) A motorcycle is a motor vehicle, other than a tractor, designed to travel on not more than three wheels in contact with the ground. 

16. T F (525) It is lawful to drive on the left side of the highway under certain conditions. 

17. T F (485) When the driver of a vehicle involved in an accident causing personal injury is unable to make the required accident report due to physical incapability, he may make the report when able just so it is not over 30 days after the accident.

Vehicle Code True and False Test for Traffic

The contents of this book are for study and guidance. Great pains have been taken to give a correct opinion in all the answers. 

We do not, however, guarantee each and every one to be exact. 

In the editor's opinion they are very accurate and with the aid of the codes will do a lot to prepare you for exams, or merely to inform you. 

1. TF Equipment specified as "special mobile equipment" is selfpropelled equipment. 

2. TFA left turn shall be made by passing immediately to the right of the center of the intersection in all cases. 

3. T F The operator's license need not be signed if always carried on your person. 

4. T F The minimum age for licensing operators is 18 years. 

5. T F It is not necessary for licensed chauffeur to have an operator's license. 

6. T F It is a crime to allow loads on passenger vehicles to extend beyond the hub caps on the left side. 

7. T F You are allowed 30 days for sending notice of change of address to the Division of Motor Vehicles. 

8. T F Loads on passenger vehicles are permitted to extend 6 inches beyond the hub caps on the right hand side. 

9. T F It is necessary to notify the police department when private garage only is rented. 

10. T F All accidents must be reported to the nearest police station or sheriff's office. 

11. T F It is a crime to fail to remove glass, or other material likely to injure tires, that you have dropped upon the highway. 

12. T F The driver of any vehicle which collides with any vehicle which is unattended who attempts to locate the owner thereof, and is unsuccessful in doing so, fully complies with the law if he leaves in a conspicuous place in the vehicle struck a written notice giving the name and address of the driver and of the owner of the vehicle doing the striking and the circumstances thereof.

13. T F In order to class a district as a business district more than 

50 per cent of the property fronting on both sides collectively of said highway for a distance of 300 feet must be occupied by buildings in use for business. 

14. T F Every public highway under the jurisdiction of the State 

Department of Public Works must be a through highway. 

15. T F Every license plate shall at all times be securely fastened to the vehicle and at a minimum distance of sixteen inches from the ground. 

16. T F Every transferee who takes possession of a motor vehicle must, within 10 days, forward both the certificate of ownership and the certificate of registration to the Motor Vehicle 

Division. 

17. T F Any person who refuses or neglects to deliver a certificate of ownership to a transferee entitled thereto is guilty of a misdemeanor. 

18. T F It is not necessary to notify the Motor Vehicle Division when a motor vehicle is leased. 

19. TF In no case does a non-resident permit allow the operation of a motor vehicle for a period exceeding 6 months. 

20. T F It is a felony to display a fictitious certifcate of registration. 

21. T F It is a felony to erase from any operator's license certificate any endorsement thereon of any conviction made by order of court. 

22. T F Automobiles may be stored in a public garage for 24 hours before the law requires that the proprietor of the garage keep a record of such storage. 

23. T F No person shall be required to obtain an operator's license for the purpose of operating an implement of husbandry temporarily propelled on the public highways. 

24. T F The minimum age of a person to whom an operator's license will be issued is 14 years. 

25. T F It shall be defense to charge of driving without a license to produce in court a license issued and valid at the time of arrest. 

26. T F Bicycles ridden on the streets at night must be equipped with a red lamp visible at least 200 feet to the rear. 

27. TFA third conviction of speeding may result in the suspension of the driver's license for a period not to exceed 12 months. 

28. TF Under ordinary conditions the maximum height allowed on any vehicle or load is thirteen feet and six inches.

29. T F The maximum legal length of any vehicle moved on any public highway is forty feet. 

30. T F School buses, vehicles carrying passengers for hire, and vehicles carrying a cargo of explosives or inflammables must stop at railroad crossings unless stop and go signals are in operation or an officer is on duty who directs to proceed. 

31. TFA vehicle approaching an intersection must yield the rightof-way to one within the intersection. 

32. TFA person who is convicted of driving while under the influence of liquor cannot drive a motor vehicle until he proves to the department that he is financially responsible. 

33. T F A vehicle entering a highway from a private road or drive must yield the right-of-way to all vehicles approaching on this highway. 

34. T F It is a crime to overtake and pass another vehicle, if in so doing your car goes onto the left side of the road while the vehicle you are overtaking is passing other vehicles coming from the opposite direction. 

35. T F A motor vehicle may coast on a mountain highway if warning sound with horn is given continuously. 

36. T F It is a crime for truck and trailer to follow another truck and trailer closer than 300 feet while on the public highway outside a business or residence district. 

37. T F It is a crime to fail to stop, give name, address, and render aid when you are involved in an accident. 

38. T F It is illegal to follow another vehicle closer than 20 feet in a business or residence district. 

39. T F The Motor Vehicle Department may not impose any restriction upon a minor to whom an operator's license is issued. 

40. T F A chauffeur's license may be issued to a married woman 

17 years of age under such restrictions as may be deemed by the Department of Motor Vehicles as appropriate to assure the safe operation of a motor vehicle by the licensee. 

41. T F The Department of Motor Vehicles shall revoke the license of any person under the age of 18 years who has been found by the Judge of the Juvenile Court to have committed reckless driving. 

42. T F No person shall knowingly permit his child, ward or employee under the age of 21 years to drive a motor vehicle. upon the highways unless such child, ward or employee is then duly licensed as an operator or chauffeur.

TEST NUMBER 1 PENAL CODE 

DIRECTIONS: Some of them are true and the others are false. If the statement is true, encircle the T, if false, encircle the F. All statements that are left unmarked will be counted as if you had marked them incorrectly.

NOTE; The following two sample questions are marked correctly. (T) F Those who aid and abet in committing a crime are principals. 

T (F) No felony has less than a five year punishment. 

IMPORTANT POINTS OF CALIFORNIA CRIMINAL LAW 

1. T F(496b) T admits knowingly receiving stolen property, namely: $51 worth of public library books found in his possession. Book him First Degree receiving stolen property. 

2. T F (487) Mary had a little lamb. John stole it. Hold John to answer on a grand theft charge. 

3. TF Joe admits stealing Mary's prize goat (Value $150). Hold 

Joe to answer on petty theft. 

4. T F(496b) There can never be any mathematical relation between the amount of a fine the Court may impose and the value of property that has been taken by the defendant illegally. 

5. TF (484) Robbery is similar to theft in at least one particular, that is each of these crimes has two degrees. 

6. T F (488a) K stole 100 pounds of deciduous fruit from L. The maximum punishment could not exceed what would be given any petty thief. 

7. T F (415) Disturbance of the Peace would be a proper charge where D and X put on a horse race, on public property, towit, a public highway, without legal permit, solely for amusement. 

8. T F (401) W admits trying to commit suicide. H admits deliberately aiding, advising and encouraging W in this matter. 

The proper procedure would be to let H be held on a felony charge, although no criminal charge should be placed against W.

Answers to Test No. 1 

Questions Nos. 1, 2, 5, 6 are true; all others are false.

TEST NUMBER 2 PENAL CODE 

1 TF (398) S, the owner of a mischievous dog, knowingly lets it run at large. The dog bites F. It is proven that the bite is the sole cause of F's death. Hold F for manslaughter. 

2. T F (518) Consent must be shown to be present in EXTOR- 

TION and shown to be absent in ROBBERY in order to secure a conviction. 

3. T F (26) If it can be proven beyond a reasonable doubt that 

Z is an ICONOCLAST, he would be considered to be a person who is incapable of committing a crime. 

4. TF (1111) A conviction can be had upon the testimony of an accomplice alone. 

5. TF (4) Our California Penal Code states that all penal statutes are to be strictly construed. 

6. TF (9) X's criminal act, for which he was convicted, caused great damage to Y. Because X is being punished prevents

Y from bringing civil damage suit against X. 

7. TF (2) In every crime or public offense there must exist a union or joint operation of act and intent or criminal negligence. 

8. T F (22) When an act has been committed by a person while in a state of voluntary intoxication, it is considered to be less criminal by reason of his having been in such condition. 

9. T F (106) P was an escape from the state prison. 

He was a lifer. The penalty for such act is to add not less than one year to his sentence. 

10. T F (127) Every person who wilfully procures another person to commit perjury is guilty of subornation of perjury, and is punishable in the same manner as he would be if personally guilty of the perjury so procured. 

11. T F (147) There is no imprisonment provided where a police officer is found guilty of wilful inhumanity or oppression toward a prisoner under his care or in his custody. 

12. T F (154) It is a felony for a debtor to remove property out of the state fraudulently, irrespective of the value of such property.

Answers to Test No. 2 

Questions Nos. 1, 4, 5, 10, 12 are true; all others are false.

OBSERVATION AND ACTION (DOUBLE SUICIDE 

CYANIDE) 

Where gas is involved in a suicide or murder, refrain from using light switch in room until gas has been removed by airing. Don't have burning cigarette in your hand, either. Observation before action is necessary when answering calls. In this case, the regular gas masks of the average fire department will not ward off "Cyanide Gas." The suicides were kind enough to have left a note warning the officers that the deadly 

Cyanide had been used.

TEST NUMBER 3 PENAL CODE 

1. TF (158 Where R has many times been convicted for assault and battery, we list him as a common barrator. 

2. T F (166) Willful disobedience of any process or order lawfully issued by any court would amount to criminal contempt. 

3. TF (171a) B took some mildly intoxicating liquor to his brother, a prisoner in our city jail. This amounts to a misdemeanor. 

4. TF (189) V, intending to commit mayhem on his wife, did slit and disfigure her face. Such act caused her death. Hold 

R for second degree murder. 

5. TF (192) A proper definition of Involuntary Manslaughter would be: The unlawful killing of a human being, without malice, upon a sudden quarrel or heat of passion. 

6. TF (212) X was an able bodied athlete who was accompanied by S, his sister, aged 16, when B, a, crippled anemic, did step up to and frighten S and rob her of certain personal property. X showed no fear. These acts are really too insignificant to indicate the kind of fear that would support a robbery charge. 

7. T F (145) It would be a misdemeanor for a policeman to willfully delay in taking a suspected prisoner before a magistrate. 

8. TF (212) Both force and fear must be proven to secure a conviction for robbery. 

9. TF (128) Procuring the execution of an innocent person by wilful subordination of perjury is punishable by death. 

10. T F (7) K admits stealing a will. Such an act could not be construed to include a codicil that was attached to the will. 

11. TF (1198) A bench warrant, if served in another county of the state need not be indorsed by a magistrate of that county.

CALIFORNIA CRIMINAL LAW

TEST NUMBER 9 PENAL CODE 

1 Only one of the following problems is correct. Mark the correct one. 

M, a minor aged 13 is charged with assault with a deadly weapon, in raining blows with a rock on O's head. A doctor testifies M has the mentality of a child of nine, but could distinguish right from wrong. M's defense is that M committed the act in a delirium of fever. 

1. The State will have to prove beyond a reasonable doubt that M knew the wrongfulness of the act. If M raised a reasonable doubt that he was unconscious of the act, M will beat the case. * * P, an absent-minded Professor who suffers spells of amnesia, was told by W his wife to throw some rubbish in the incinerator and put their baby in the baby carriage. P in a trance puts the baby in the incinerator and the rubbish in the carriage. The baby dies of suffocation. 

2. This may mean death. 

F, a prize fighter was knocked out in a ring battle. When he came to he was raving about the fight. F seized a razor and cut 

M, his manager. 

3. Unless F can establish insanity, he has no defense. 2 S, a sailor, while in Australia, obtained a sap-stick. While S is in foreign waters, California passed a law making it a felony to have possession or control of a sap-stick. S, on landing in San Pedro is arrested. In his room at a hotel is found the sap-stick, among his belongings. 

1. S may defend here on the ground he had no guilty intent. 

2. S committed no guilty act here. 

3. There would have to be more proof of S's intent to violate the law. 

4. S is guilty here.
